 2. A. 10.15.19 
2. RECOGNITION 
A. TSD Mission Moment – George Freeman 
George Freeman is a computer tech at Hill and Morse Elementary Schools.  This 
summer during an unexpected power outage at Boulan Park Middle School, George 
came in to save the day. Invite Natalie Haezebrouck, Director of Teaching & 
Learning, up to introduce George and share more about his selfless efforts.   
Recommended Action:  Distribute certificates of appreciation.

2. B. 10.15.19 
2. RECOGNITION 
B.  2020 National Merit Scholarship Semifinalists 
This year, more than one million students, nationwide, from some 22,000 high 
schools, entered the National Merit Scholarship Program; however, only 16,000 of 
these seniors are selected to be semifinalists. This evening, we will be passing a 
resolution to commend and congratulate our fifty -six National Merit Scholarship 
Semifinalists: Lillian Adams, Anisha Aggarwal (Uh -knee-sha Uh -grr-wall), Divya 
Aggarwal (Div-ya Uh-ger-wall), Fareeha Baig (Fa -ree-ha Bayg), Rohit Bollampally 
(Row-hit Bo-lam-pal-e), Arlene Chen, Edison Chiu (Edison Chew), Atticus Chong (At- 
i-kuhs Chong), John Dannug (John Dan-nuhg), Balraj Dhanoa (Ball-raaj Duh-no-ah), 
Angela Dong, Matthew Dong, Priya Dutta ( Pree-ya Duh-ta), Julia Genord (Jew-lee- 
uh Jen-ord), Sreya Gogineni (Sray -ah Go-gee-nay-nee), Amogh Gowda (Uh -MOAG 
GOW-duh), Jimming He, Caitlin Henning, Catherine Hu, Nithya Jaisankar (Nith -ya 
Jay-shunk-er), Nidhi Jaison (Nid -ee Jay -son), Katherine Jean, Katherine Lee, 
Christina Li (Christina Lee), Claire Li (Claire Lee), Richard Li (Richard Lee), Shannon 
Li (Shannon Lee), Yusra Mahmood (You -sra Ma-mood), Dhatri Medarametla (Dah - 
tree Meh -dah-rah-met-la), Aakash Menon (Ahh -ka-sh Men -on), Rithvik 
Mohanarangan, Dhruv Muralidhar (Dh -roov Moor-leader), Dixsheta Muralikrishnan 
(Dik-she-tuh Mu-rah-lee-krish-nun), Hareesh Ragav (Her -EE-shh Rug-ov), Shrutee 
Rakshit (Shroo-tee Ruck-shith), Danyal Raza (Dan-yul Raa-zah), Laya Sathyan (Lai- 
yuh Sah-thee-yah-n), Gurish Sharma (Gur-eesh Sharma), Addy Shaska, Natania Shi 
(Natania Shoo), Pratham Soni (Pruh -thum So -knee), Krishay Sridalla (Krish -aye 
Sree-dah-la), Shanan Sun (Shannon Sun), Shruthi Sunilkumar (Shroo -tee Sue-neil- 
koo-mar), Lydia Tan, Apurva Vaddaman i (Uh-per-va Vah-da-mah-knee), Mahimna 
Vyas (Muh-him-nuh Ve-yaas), Kevin Wang (Kevin W-ong), Jennifer Won, Emily Wu, 
Ailin Xiong (Eye -lean Sh-oo-ng), Sophia Yee, Carol Zhang, Caroline Zhang, Aimee 
Zheng, and Kevin Zhou (Kevin Jo).  This is a remarkable number of semifinalists, and 
a tribute to the staff and parents of the Troy School District. Copies of this resolution 
will be presented to the students at a reception on October 30th. 
RECOMMENDATION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
MOVED BY

RESOLUTION 
4
2020 
National Merit Scholarship Semifinalists 
Whereas, the above-mentioned fifty-six students earned the prestigious honor of being 
among those selected, nationwide, as this year's National Merit Scholarship Semifinalists: 
Whereas, student achievement is honored through the National Merit Scholarship 
semifinalist and finalist recognition programs; and 
Whereas, National Merit students reach semifinalist status by scoring in the top one 
and one-half percent on the National Preliminary Scholastic Aptitude Test (PSAT) and 
earning comparable scores on the Scholastic Aptitude Test (SAT). 
Be It Therefore Resolved that the Troy School District Board of Education adopts this 
resolution in honor of the aforementioned Troy School District students for their 
selection as 2020 National Merit Scholarship Semifinalists. 
Signed this fifteenth day of October, 2019.

NO

Certificate  of  Excellence  in  Financial  Reporting  Program 
honored  the  Troy  School  District  for  excellence  in  the 
preparation  and  issuance  of  a  high  quality  Comprehensive 
Annual Financial Report (CAFR) for the fiscal year ended June 
30, 2018.  The Certificate of Excellence in Financial Reporting  
has  been  created  by  ASBO  International  to  enable  business 
officials to achieve a high standard of financial reporting. We  
a r e  v e r y  p l e a s e d  t o  r e c o g n i z e  R i c k  W e s t ,  A s s i s t a n t  
Superintendent  for  Business  Services,  and  his  hard‐working 
staff  for  all  their  effort  throughout  the  year  and  for  their 
continued dedication to excellence.

Community College.  This is the second semester that Noah

career in home inspection.  Noah is a member of Student

o t h e r s .   T h i s  s c h o o l  l e a d e r  a l s o  w o r k s  p a r t ‐ t i m e  a n d  i s  a  
member of his church youth group. 
Pranay Gupta, International Academy – East  
Praynay Gupta is a junior at International Academy East. He 
is  actively  involved  in  the  school’s  robotics  team,  Forensics 
club,  and  the  school’s  symphony  and  full  orchestras.  His 
hobbies  include  programming,  building  electronics,  zip‐
lining, rock climbing, and helping others.  
Noah Scudder, Athens High School 
Noah Scudder is a senior at Athens High School.  Noah is an 
accomplished scholar, leader, and musician. He is currently 
serving as the President of Athens Student Congress, Head 
Drum  Major  of  the  Red  Hawk  Marching  Band,  a 
Representative  on  the  Superintendent’s  Student  Advisory 
Board (SSAB), and a Board Member of Athens’ French Club. 
Noah has also interned for U.S. Representative Haley

Banasik,  legal  counsel  for  MASB  (Michigan  Association  of 
School Boards) regarding polling members of the school board 
through  email  and  whether  it  was  in  violation  of  the  OMA 
(Open Meetings Act).  Since it was stated in the email “defer 
to majority of board members” it was considered a violation 
of the OMA.  To rectify this, it was recommended the items be 
introduced at this evening’s meeting and voted on whether to 
remove the item from the agenda.   President Schmidt asked 
for a motion to remove the agenda items under Business. 
Mrs.  Hammond  moved  to  remove  items  8A  and  8B  under 
Business on the agenda.

CURRICULUM 
shared  a  PowerPoint  presentation  on  the  Professional 
Learning opportunities offered to teachers and administrators 
throughout the summer.  She tal ked about the interest and 
success  of  over  100  sessions  offered  this  year,  which  were 
presented  to  teachers  by  their  own  colleagues  themselves. 
She  recognized  Christine  DiPilato,  Assistant  Superintendent, 
Secondary Instruction, and Nata lie Haezebrouck, Director of 
T e a c h i n g  &  L e a r n i n g ,  f o r  a l l  t h e  w o r k  t h e y  p u t  i n t o  t h i s  
Professional  Learning.    Ms.  Griffor  also  shared  about  the 
superintendent’s  opening  day  welcome  and  how  well 
received the keynote speakers were.  They were TSD teachers 
and  students  who  shared  their  own  personal  learning 
experiences in their classrooms. 
There was some Board discussion following the presentation. 
provided  an  update  on  the  District’s  enrollment  status  and 
thanked the Central Enrollment team for all their hard work 
in  enrolling  the  many  new  families  who  have  joined  the 
District.  She reported on the District’s enrollment numbers as 
of today but indicated that these numbers are still changing 
daily.

Mr. Schmidt provided each Board member the opportunity to 
share  their  thoughts  on  this  proposed  purchase  of  the  ITT 
Building on Big Beaver Road to be the future location of TCCHS 
and Niles’ programs since the Niles Building has reached its 
end‐of‐life use. 
Dr. Wilson thanked Mr. West for his due‐diligence and shared 
t h a t  s h e  w a s  e x c i t e d  f o r  t h e  a c q u i s i t i o n  o f  t h e  b u i l d i n g  a n d  
property and is glad to see that our Adult Education program 
is valued and that we can enhance their workspace in the new 
location. 
Mrs.  Hammond  asked  to  have  this  item  removed  from  the 
agenda because she hasn’t been given enough information to 
make an informed decision.  She commented on the TCCHS 
program  which  runs  at  a  deficit  and  the  cost  of  the  new 
preschool building.  She indicated that she isn’t 100% against 
this  but  wants  to  look  at  all  possibilities  in  the  District  and 
asked  to  bring  back  a  Facilities  Study  Team  to re‐assess  the 
District buildings for space again.   
Mrs. Fleming thanked Mr. West for all his hard work on this 
project  and  shared  she  thinks  the  ITT  Building  would  be 
perfect for TCCHS and Adult E ducation and has confidence in 
administration’s recommendation.    
Mr. Hauff thanked Mr. West, Dr. Machesky and the staff for 
their hard work on this project and thinks Big Beaver, named 
t h e  G o l d e n  C o r r i d o r  b y  C i t y  o f f i c i a l s ,  w o u l d  b e  p e r f e c t  t o  
service the TCCHS students. 
Dr. Philippart shared that in addition to being the right thing

to do.  The ITT Building is suited for educational use and could

Mr. Schmidt agreed that Niles is falling apart and if the District 
waited  until  the  next  bond  proposal,  the  building  wouldn’t

open  until  2026.    ITT  is  built  as  an  instructional  space  and

Mrs.  Hammond  offered  a  rebuttal  talking  about  the  District

public to help advocate for funding public education.

WHEREAS,  Section  1351(2)  of  Act  451,  Public  Acts  of 
Michigan,  1976,  as  amended  (“Act 451”),  authorizes  the 
Board of Education of a school district to borrow money and 
issue bonds or notes without the vote of the school electors 
if the total outstanding bonded indebtedness of the school 
district is less than 5% of the state equalized valuation of the 
taxable property within the district; and 
WHEREAS,  the  Board  deems  it  necessary  to  purchase  real 
property; and 
WHEREAS, it is deemed advisable to authorize the issuance 
of bonds in the aggregate principal amount not to exceed Six 
Million One Hundred Thousand Dollars ($6,100,000), or such 
lesser amount as shall be determined by the Superintendent 
or Assistant Superintendent of Business Services, to pay the 
costs  of  the  purchase  and  renovation  of  the  property,  and 
costs of issuance of the bonds; and

A. CONSIDERATION:  NSBA Advocacy Institute Conference  
As required by Board Policy 1000.01, Organization and Functioning of the 
Board; Reimbursement of Expenses, “Any expenditure of District funds for 
members for out -of-state meetings must have prior approval by 
resolution.”  Board Secretary Gary Hauff and Board Trustees Steve 
Gottlieb and Nicole Wilson  have indicated their desire to attend the 
National School Boards Association (NSBA) Advocacy Institute Conference, 
which will be held in Washington, D.C., from February 1  - 4, 20 20. 
Therefore, the Board is being asked to consider the following resolution.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution.

   Moved by  
   Supported by

Hauff, Mr. Gottlieb, and Dr. Wilson’s attendance as representative s of the Troy 
Board of Education at the NSBA Advocacy Institute  Conference in Wash ington, 
D.C., from February 1 - 4, 2020.

  10. B.  10.15.19 
10. OTHER 
B.  CONSIDERATION:   RESOLUTION REGARDING INTERNATIONAL 
ACADEMY (OAKLAND) 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Approve the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: ______________________________ 
Supported By: ______________________________ 
 WHEREAS, the District is a Participating District in the International Academy 
(“IA”) and is a party to the Consortium Agreement that sets forth the terms and 
conditions by which the IA is governed and operates; and 
 WHEREAS, at the October 2, 2019 meeting of the IA’s Joint Steering Committee 
(“JSC”), the JSC adopted a Resolution approving an amendment to the governance 
structure of the IA as set forth in the Consortium Agreement; and 
 WHEREAS, the Resolution the JSC adopted stated as follows: 
1. The JSC approves Alternative Number 1 attached to the September 25, 
2019 correspondence from Board Presidents Schmidt and Wiseman. 
2. The JSC requests that each JSC Member advise its Board  of Education of 
this resolution, and to advise its Board of Education that it is the JSC’s 
recommendation that the Consortium move forward with the Alternative 
Number 1 governance option. 
3. The JSC requests that each JSC Member present a resolution to its  board 
of Education which ratifies the JSC recommendation and approves the 
Alternative Number 1 governance option. 
4. All prior resolutions and parts of prior resolutions insofar as they conflict 
with the provisions of this resolution be and the same are her eby 
rescinded. 
  WHEREAS, the Board deems it to be in the best interests of the District to ratify 
the JSC recommendation and approve the governance option adopted by the JSC;   
141
  10. B.  10.15.19 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 1. The Board hereby ratifies the above stated JSC recommendation and 
approves the governance option adopted by the JSC, which is as follows: 
The Consortium shall be governed by a five (5) member body consisting 
of the duly appointed representatives of its three (3) Fiscal Agents and two 
(2) additional at-large members elected by majority vote of all Participating 
Districts (“Governing Body”).  With respect to the initial election of the two 
(2) at -large members, the three (3) Fiscal Agent representatives shall 
create a ballot with the names of the Superintendents (or their designees) 
of all Participating Districts that wish to have representation on the 
Governing Body and disseminate the ballot to the Participating Districts’ 
boards of education for vote.  Placement on the ballot shall req uire a 
resolution of a Participating District’s board of education designating the 
district’s Superintendent (or his/her designee) as that district’s proposed 
representative as an at-large member of the Governing Body.  The election 
shall take place as soo n as practicable after the effective date of this 
Amendment.  The Fiscal Agents shall be entitled to cast votes for at -large 
members. 
In the event that only one of the proposed at -large members of the 
Governing Body on the ballot receives a majority of vo tes by the 
Participating Districts, a new ballot shall be created by the three (3) Fiscal 
Agent Representatives with the names of the proposed representatives 
who received the two (2) next highest number of votes and a new election 
shall take place as soon  as practicable to select the remaining at -large 
member.  In the event that none of the proposed at -large members 
receives a majority of votes, a new ballot shall be created by the three (3) 
Fiscal Agent representatives comprised of the names of the three (3) 
proposed representatives who receive the highest number of votes, and a 
new election shall take place whereby the two (2) proposed 
representatives who receive the highest number of votes shall be selected 
as at-large members of the Governing Body.  In the case of a tie vote for 
one of the two membership positions, a runoff election shall be held as 
soon as practicable. 
The Fiscal Agent representatives shall serve as permanent members of the 
Governing Body, subject to the separation of their employment with a 
Fiscal Agent and the appointment of a new representative by a duly 
adopted resolution of the Fiscal Agent’s board of education.  The at -large 
members of the Governing Body shall be elected to and shall serve terms 
of two (2) years.  Subsequent to the initial election of the two (2) at-large 
members, the entire Governing Body shall be responsible for administering 
the at-large member election process described herein. 
142
  10. B.  10.15.19 
2. All other resolutions and parts of resolutions insofar as they conflict with 
the provisions of this resolution are hereby rescinded. 
YES: MEMBERS:           
NO: MEMBERS:
